  "meta_commentary": "**Critical execution guidance:**\n\n**Import ordering is the single highest-risk aspect.** The wgp/torch/huggingface_hub stubs MUST be in sys.modules before ANY `from source.runtime.worker import server` or similar. The run_preview.py script should set up all stubs in a top-level block, then do all source/ imports inside a `def main()` or after the stub setup. If any source/ import happens at module level before stubs, server.py's `import wgp as wgp_mod` will fail.\n\n**Monkeypatching correctness is the second-highest risk.** Python's `from X import Y` binds `Y` in the importing module's namespace. Patching `X.Y` after import does nothing \u2014 you must patch `importing_module.Y`. For each of the ~18 monkeypatches, the executor MUST grep for the function name to find where it's imported and patch at that location. The most common mistake: patching `source.core.db.task_claim.poll_next_task` when server.py has already done `from source.core.db.task_claim import poll_next_task` \u2014 the server's local binding is unaffected.\n\n**SpoofDbRuntime is the most complex new component.** The orchestrator handlers (travel_orchestrator, join_clips_orchestrator) depend on exact dict shapes from `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()`. Read the handler code to see what keys it accesses. This is where the orchestrator flow will break if the shape is wrong.\n\n**SpoofQueue's 22-attribute surface:** Don't trust the plan's list blindly. Read task_processor.py:60-216 and task_processor.py:307-570 line by line, noting every `queue.X` or `self.X` (where self is the queue). Add any missing attributes. The plan's list is a starting point, not exhaustive.\n\n**Orchestrator child-task flow:** This is the most complex runtime behavior. After `travel_orchestrator` processes, it calls `add_task_to_db()` for children. SpoofDbRuntime stores them. SpoofTaskFeed must detect them on the next poll and serve them in dependency order. Then each child processes and completes. This multi-step flow is where integration bugs will concentrate.\n\n**Fixture accuracy:** Copy param structures from create_test_task.py exactly, then sanitize URLs. Don't invent params. Invalid param structures will cause silent failures or crashes deep in task processing.\n\n**No source/ modifications:** If you find yourself needing to change source/ files, STOP and find a monkeypatch approach instead. The harness must be entirely additive.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: the attached success criteria are stale and do not match the current plan body. they still require 'no `usestate` for `activepairdata`', '`setactivepairdata` fully removed', 'sync effect deleted', and '`onpairclick` simplified to `(pairindex: number) => void`', which are the opposite of the current targeted-sync plan. as presented, the criteria are not usable for review or execution. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

  "meta_commentary": "**Critical execution guidance:**\n\n**Import ordering is the single highest-risk aspect.** The wgp/torch/huggingface_hub stubs MUST be in sys.modules before ANY `from source.runtime.worker import server` or similar. The run_preview.py script should set up all stubs in a top-level block, then do all source/ imports inside a `def main()` or after the stub setup. If any source/ import happens at module level before stubs, server.py's `import wgp as wgp_mod` will fail.\n\n**Monkeypatching correctness is the second-highest risk.** Python's `from X import Y` binds `Y` in the importing module's namespace. Patching `X.Y` after import does nothing \u2014 you must patch `importing_module.Y`. For each of the ~18 monkeypatches, the executor MUST grep for the function name to find where it's imported and patch at that location. The most common mistake: patching `source.core.db.task_claim.poll_next_task` when server.py has already done `from source.core.db.task_claim import poll_next_task` \u2014 the server's local binding is unaffected.\n\n**SpoofDbRuntime is the most complex new component.** The orchestrator handlers (travel_orchestrator, join_clips_orchestrator) depend on exact dict shapes from `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()`. Read the handler code to see what keys it accesses. This is where the orchestrator flow will break if the shape is wrong.\n\n**SpoofQueue's 22-attribute surface:** Don't trust the plan's list blindly. Read task_processor.py:60-216 and task_processor.py:307-570 line by line, noting every `queue.X` or `self.X` (where self is the queue). Add any missing attributes. The plan's list is a starting point, not exhaustive.\n\n**Orchestrator child-task flow:** This is the most complex runtime behavior. After `travel_orchestrator` processes, it calls `add_task_to_db()` for children. SpoofDbRuntime stores them. SpoofTaskFeed must detect them on the next poll and serve them in dependency order. Then each child processes and completes. This multi-step flow is where integration bugs will concentrate.\n\n**Fixture accuracy:** Copy param structures from create_test_task.py exactly, then sanitize URLs. Don't invent params. Invalid param structures will cause silent failures or crashes deep in task processing.\n\n**No source/ modifications:** If you find yourself needing to change source/ files, STOP and find a monkeypatch approach instead. The harness must be entirely additive.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: the attached success criteria are stale and do not match the current plan body. they still require 'no `usestate` for `activepairdata`', '`setactivepairdata` fully removed', 'sync effect deleted', and '`onpairclick` simplified to `(pairindex: number) => void`', which are the opposite of the current targeted-sync plan. as presented, the criteria are not usable for review or execution. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

exc:\n+                headless_logger.error(\n+                    f\"Unhandled exception while processing task {current_task_id}: {exc}\",\n+                    task_id=current_task_id,\n+                    exc_info=True,\n+                )\n+                error_message = str(exc) or exc.__class__.__name__\n+                is_retryable, error_category, max_attempts = is_retryable_error(error_message)\n+                current_attempts = task_info.get(\"attempts\", 0)\n+                if is_retryable and current_attempts < max_attempts:\n+                    spoof_db.requeue(current_task_id, error_message, current_attempts, error_category)\n+                else:\n+                    spoof_db.update_status_complete(current_task_id, STATUS_FAILED, error_message)\n+            finally:\n+                set_current_task_context(None)\n+                display.on_task_done()\n+\n+        summary = \", \".join(\n+            f\"{status}={count}\" for status, count in sorted(_summarize_statuses(spoof_db).items())\n+        ) or \"no tasks\"\n+        headless_logger.essential(f\"[PREVIEW] Preview harness complete: {summary}\")\n+        return 0\n+    except FatalWorkerError as exc:\n+        headless_logger.critical(f\"[PREVIEW] Fatal worker error: {exc}\")\n+        return 1\n+\n+\n+def main(argv: Iterable[str] | None = None) -> int:\n+    args = parse_args(argv)\n+    if not args.output_log:\n+        return run_preview(argv)\n+\n+    log_path = Path(args.output_log)\n+    log_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n+    with log_path.open(\"w\", encoding=\"utf-8\") as log_file:\n+        tee_stdout = TeeStream(sys.stdout, log_file)\n+        tee_stderr = TeeStream(sys.stderr, log_file)\n+        with contextlib.redirect_stdout(tee_stdout), contextlib.redirect_stderr(tee_stderr):\n+            return run_preview(argv)\n+\n+\n+if __name__ == \"__main__\":\n+    raise SystemExit(main())\n*** End Patch\n"
}

  "meta_commentary": "**Critical execution guidance:**\n\n**Import ordering is the single highest-risk aspect.** The wgp/torch/huggingface_hub stubs MUST be in sys.modules before ANY `from source.runtime.worker import server` or similar. The run_preview.py script should set up all stubs in a top-level block, then do all source/ imports inside a `def main()` or after the stub setup. If any source/ import happens at module level before stubs, server.py's `import wgp as wgp_mod` will fail.\n\n**Monkeypatching correctness is the second-highest risk.** Python's `from X import Y` binds `Y` in the importing module's namespace. Patching `X.Y` after import does nothing \u2014 you must patch `importing_module.Y`. For each of the ~18 monkeypatches, the executor MUST grep for the function name to find where it's imported and patch at that location. The most common mistake: patching `source.core.db.task_claim.poll_next_task` when server.py has already done `from source.core.db.task_claim import poll_next_task` \u2014 the server's local binding is unaffected.\n\n**SpoofDbRuntime is the most complex new component.** The orchestrator handlers (travel_orchestrator, join_clips_orchestrator) depend on exact dict shapes from `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()`. Read the handler code to see what keys it accesses. This is where the orchestrator flow will break if the shape is wrong.\n\n**SpoofQueue's 22-attribute surface:** Don't trust the plan's list blindly. Read task_processor.py:60-216 and task_processor.py:307-570 line by line, noting every `queue.X` or `self.X` (where self is the queue). Add any missing attributes. The plan's list is a starting point, not exhaustive.\n\n**Orchestrator child-task flow:** This is the most complex runtime behavior. After `travel_orchestrator` processes, it calls `add_task_to_db()` for children. SpoofDbRuntime stores them. SpoofTaskFeed must detect them on the next poll and serve them in dependency order. Then each child processes and completes. This multi-step flow is where integration bugs will concentrate.\n\n**Fixture accuracy:** Copy param structures from create_test_task.py exactly, then sanitize URLs. Don't invent params. Invalid param structures will cause silent failures or crashes deep in task processing.\n\n**No source/ modifications:** If you find yourself needing to change source/ files, STOP and find a monkeypatch approach instead. The harness must be entirely additive.",

        Debt watch items (do not make these worse):
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the fix is incomplete for extra trailing slots. `handlepairclick` can populate `activepairdata` from `trailingpairdata` when `pairindex === pairdatabyindex.size` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l121), but the proposed sync effect only reads `pairdatabyindex.get(segmentslotlightboxindex)`. for those trailing-slot cases `fresh` is `undefined`, so no image refresh happens. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-success-criteria-well-prioritized-and-verifiable: are the success criteria well-prioritized and verifiable?: the attached success criteria are stale and do not match the current plan body. they still require 'no `usestate` for `activepairdata`', '`setactivepairdata` fully removed', 'sync effect deleted', and '`onpairclick` simplified to `(pairindex: number) => void`', which are the opposite of the current targeted-sync plan. as presented, the criteria are not usable for review or execution. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

{
  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Update File: /Users/user_c042661f/Documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-worker/scripts/preview/db_spoof.py\n@@\n class SpoofDbRuntime:\n@@\n     def _task_entry(self, record: _TaskRecord) -> dict[str, Any]:\n         params = copy.deepcopy(record.params)\n         return {\n@@\n             \"created_at\": f\"preview-{record.created_index:04d}\",\n         }\n+\n+    def _requeue_parent_orchestrator(self, record: _TaskRecord) -> None:\n+        parent_id = self._orchestrator_ref(record.params)\n+        if not parent_id:\n+            return\n+        parent = self._records.get(parent_id)\n+        if parent is None or parent.status in TERMINAL_STATUSES or parent.status == \"Queued\":\n+            return\n+        parent.status = \"Queued\"\n+        self._log(\n+            f\"[SPOOF_DB] Requeued parent {parent_id} after child {record.task_id} reached {record.status}\"\n+        )\n@@\n     def update_task_status(self, task_id: str, status: str, output_location: str | None = None):\n         record = self._records[task_id]\n         record.status = status\n         record.output_location = output_location\n         self._log(f\"[SPOOF_DB] {task_id} -> {status}: {output_location}\")\n+        if status in TERMINAL_STATUSES:\n+            self._requeue_parent_orchestrator(record)\n         return True\n \n     def update_task_status_supabase(self, task_id: str, status: str, output_location: str | None = None, thumbnail_url: str | None = None):\n         record = self._records[task_id]\n         record.status = status\n         record.output_location = output_location\n         record.thumbnail_url = thumbnail_url\n         self._log(f\"[SPOOF_DB] Complete: {task_id} -> {status}: {output_location}\")\n+        if status in TERMINAL_STATUSES:\n+            self._requeue_parent_orchestrator(record)\n         return True\n*** End Patch\n"

SpoofDbRuntime** \u2014 in-memory task graph supporting the full DB surface used by orchestrator handlers:\n- `add_task_to_db(payload, type, dependant_on)` \u2192 assigns UUID, stores task with status `Queued`, records dependencies, returns UUID\n- `get_orchestrator_child_tasks(orch_id)` \u2192 returns categorized dict of children matching the real return shape (`segments`, `stitch`, `join_clips_segment`, etc.)\n- `get_task_dependency(task_id)` \u2192 returns stored `dependant_on`\n- `get_task_current_status(task_id)` \u2192 returns current status string\n- `cancel_orchestrator_children(orch_id)` \u2192 sets all children to `Cancelled`\n- `cleanup_duplicate_child_tasks(orch_id)` \u2192 no-op\n- `get_task_output_location_from_db(task_id)` \u2192 returns stored output_location for completed tasks\n- `update_task_status(task_id, status, output)` \u2192 updates in-memory, logs `[SPOOF_DB]`\n- `update_task_status_supabase(task_id, status, output, thumbnail)` \u2192 same + logs `[SPOOF_DB] Complete:`\n- `reset_generation_started_at(task_id)` \u2192 no-op with debug log\n- `requeue_task_for_retry(task_id, ...)` \u2192 sets status back to `Queued`\n\nIMPORTANT: Read `source/core/db/task_completion.py`, `source/core/db/task_dependencies.py`, and `source/core/db/task_polling.py` to verify exact function signatures and return shapes before implementing. The orchestrator handlers depend on the exact dict structure returned by `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()` \u2014 get this wrong and orchestrators will crash.\n\n**2b. SpoofTaskFeed** replacing `poll_next_task`:\n- Holds `SpoofDbRuntime` reference\n- On poll: finds next ready task (status `Queued`, all dependencies satisfied)\n- Returns `(ClaimPollOutcome.CLAIMED, task_info)` for ready tasks\n- Returns `(ClaimPollOutcome.EMPTY, None)` for idle polls (configurable count between tasks, default: 1)\n- Returns `(None, None)` sentinel when all tasks are terminal and no more can become ready\n- Handles orchestrator child-task flow: root orchestators create children during processing \u2192 children become ready \u2192 feed serves them\n\n**2c.** Inject DB config: set `source.core.db.config.SUPABASE_URL = \"https://preview.supabase.local\"` and `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN = \"preview-token\"`.",

SpoofDbRuntime** \u2014 in-memory task graph supporting the full DB surface used by orchestrator handlers:\n- `add_task_to_db(payload, type, dependant_on)` \u2192 assigns UUID, stores task with status `Queued`, records dependencies, returns UUID\n- `get_orchestrator_child_tasks(orch_id)` \u2192 returns categorized dict of children matching the real return shape (`segments`, `stitch`, `join_clips_segment`, etc.)\n- `get_task_dependency(task_id)` \u2192 returns stored `dependant_on`\n- `get_task_current_status(task_id)` \u2192 returns current status string\n- `cancel_orchestrator_children(orch_id)` \u2192 sets all children to `Cancelled`\n- `cleanup_duplicate_child_tasks(orch_id)` \u2192 no-op\n- `get_task_output_location_from_db(task_id)` \u2192 returns stored output_location for completed tasks\n- `update_task_status(task_id, status, output)` \u2192 updates in-memory, logs `[SPOOF_DB]`\n- `update_task_status_supabase(task_id, status, output, thumbnail)` \u2192 same + logs `[SPOOF_DB] Complete:`\n- `reset_generation_started_at(task_id)` \u2192 no-op with debug log\n- `requeue_task_for_retry(task_id, ...)` \u2192 sets status back to `Queued`\n\nIMPORTANT: Read `source/core/db/task_completion.py`, `source/core/db/task_dependencies.py`, and `source/core/db/task_polling.py` to verify exact function signatures and return shapes before implementing. The orchestrator handlers depend on the exact dict structure returned by `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()` \u2014 get this wrong and orchestrators will crash.\n\n**2b. SpoofTaskFeed** replacing `poll_next_task`:\n- Holds `SpoofDbRuntime` reference\n- On poll: finds next ready task (status `Queued`, all dependencies satisfied)\n- Returns `(ClaimPollOutcome.CLAIMED, task_info)` for ready tasks\n- Returns `(ClaimPollOutcome.EMPTY, None)` for idle polls (configurable count between tasks, default: 1)\n- Returns `(None, None)` sentinel when all tasks are terminal and no more can become ready\n- Handles orchestrator child-task flow: root orchestators create children during processing \u2192 children become ready \u2192 feed serves them\n\n**2c.** Inject DB config: set `source.core.db.config.SUPABASE_URL = \"https://preview.supabase.local\"` and `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN = \"preview-token\"`.",

      "description": "Create `reigh-worker/scripts/preview/run_preview.py` \u2014 the main preview runner.\n\n**6a. Early environment setup (BEFORE any `source/` imports):**\n- Set `HEADLESS_WAN2GP_SMOKE=1`, `HEADLESS_WAN2GP_FORCE_CPU=1`\n- Call `inject_module_stubs()` from `wgp_spoof.py`\n- Inject DB config values from `db_spoof.py`\n- Call `ensure_assets()` from `assets.py`\n\n**6b. Controlled imports (AFTER stubs in place):**\n- `source.core.log`: `enable_debug_mode`, `disable_debug_mode`, `headless_logger`, `set_current_task_context`\n- `source.runtime.worker.server`: `bootstrap_runtime_environment`, `process_single_task`\n- `source.runtime.worker.status_display`: `WorkerStatusDisplay`\n- `source.core.db.task_claim`: `ClaimPollOutcome`\n- `source.core.params.task_result`: `TaskResult`, `TaskOutcome`\n\n**6c. Monkeypatch spoofs** \u2014 CRITICAL: patch at the module where the function is IMPORTED, not where it's DEFINED. Read each import site in server.py/task_processor.py/task_registry.py to find the correct patch target:\n- `source.core.db.task_claim.poll_next_task` \u2192 `SpoofTaskFeed.poll`\n- `source.core.db.task_status.update_task_status_supabase` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.update_status_complete`\n- `source.core.db.task_status.update_task_status` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.update_status`\n- `source.core.db.task_status.reset_generation_started_at` \u2192 no-op\n- `source.core.db.lifecycle.task_status_retry.requeue_task_for_retry` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.requeue`\n- `source.core.db.task_completion.add_task_to_db` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.add_task`\n- `source.core.db.task_dependencies.get_orchestrator_child_tasks` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.get_children`\n- `source.core.db.task_dependencies.get_task_dependency` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.get_dependency`\n- `source.core.db.task_dependencies.get_task_current_status` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.get_status`\n- `source.core.db.task_dependencies.cancel_orchestrator_children` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.cancel_children`\n- `source.core.db.task_dependencies.cleanup_duplicate_child_tasks` \u2192 no-op\n- `source.core.db.task_polling.get_task_output_location_from_db` \u2192 `SpoofDbRuntime.get_output`\n- `source.models.lora.lora_utils.download_lora_from_url` \u2192 local path stub\n- `source.utils.download_utils.download_file` \u2192 local path stub\n- `source.utils.download_utils.download_image_if_url` \u2192 local path stub\n- `source.runtime.worker.guardian.send_heartbeat_with_logs` \u2192 no-op\n- `source.task_handlers.worker.heartbeat_utils.start_heartbeat_guardian_process` \u2192 returns `(None, None)`\n- `source.task_handlers.worker.worker_utils.cleanup_generated_files` \u2192 no-op\n\nAlso check: if any of these functions are imported with `from X import Y` in the calling module, you must patch on the CALLING module, not on X. Grep for each function name to find all import sites.\n\n**6d. Main loop** mirroring `server.py:442-583`:\n```python\nbootstrap_runtime_environment()\nif args.debug:\n    enable_debug_mode()\nelse:\n    disable_debug_mode()\n\ndisplay = WorkerStatusDisplay(\"Preview GPU (spoofed)\", \"Preview Profile\")\ndisplay.show_banner()\nspoof_db = SpoofDbRuntime(fixtures)\nspoof_queue = SpoofQueue(wan_dir=wan2gp_path, db_runtime=spoof_db)\n\nwhile True:\n    outcome, task_info = poll_next_task(worker_id=\"preview-worker\", ...)\n    if outcome is None:  # sentinel: all tasks terminal\n        break\n    if outcome == ClaimPollOutcome.EMPTY:\n        display.show_idle()\n        time.sleep(0.5)  # compressed idle\n        continue\n    display.on_task_start()\n    raw_result = process_single_task(...)\n    # status logging mirrors server.py:496-547\n    # For orchestrator tasks: update SpoofDbRuntime so children become ready\n    display.on_task_done()\n```\n\n**6e. CLI flags:** `--task-types` (comma filter), `--debug`/`--normal` (default: normal), `--output-log PATH`, `--idle-polls N` (default: 1)\n\n**6f. Termination:** log summary and exit cleanly after feed exhausted.",

  "meta_commentary": "**Critical execution guidance:**\n\n**Import ordering is the single highest-risk aspect.** The wgp/torch/huggingface_hub stubs MUST be in sys.modules before ANY `from source.runtime.worker import server` or similar. The run_preview.py script should set up all stubs in a top-level block, then do all source/ imports inside a `def main()` or after the stub setup. If any source/ import happens at module level before stubs, server.py's `import wgp as wgp_mod` will fail.\n\n**Monkeypatching correctness is the second-highest risk.** Python's `from X import Y` binds `Y` in the importing module's namespace. Patching `X.Y` after import does nothing \u2014 you must patch `importing_module.Y`. For each of the ~18 monkeypatches, the executor MUST grep for the function name to find where it's imported and patch at that location. The most common mistake: patching `source.core.db.task_claim.poll_next_task` when server.py has already done `from source.core.db.task_claim import poll_next_task` \u2014 the server's local binding is unaffected.\n\n**SpoofDbRuntime is the most complex new component.** The orchestrator handlers (travel_orchestrator, join_clips_orchestrator) depend on exact dict shapes from `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()`. Read the handler code to see what keys it accesses. This is where the orchestrator flow will break if the shape is wrong.\n\n**SpoofQueue's 22-attribute surface:** Don't trust the plan's list blindly. Read task_processor.py:60-216 and task_processor.py:307-570 line by line, noting every `queue.X` or `self.X` (where self is the queue). Add any missing attributes. The plan's list is a starting point, not exhaustive.\n\n**Orchestrator child-task flow:** This is the most complex runtime behavior. After `travel_orchestrator` processes, it calls `add_task_to_db()` for children. SpoofDbRuntime stores them. SpoofTaskFeed must detect them on the next poll and serve them in dependency order. Then each child processes and completes. This multi-step flow is where integration bugs will concentrate.\n\n**Fixture accuracy:** Copy param structures from create_test_task.py exactly, then sanitize URLs. Don't invent params. Invalid param structures will cause silent failures or crashes deep in task processing.\n\n**No source/ modifications:** If you find yourself needing to change source/ files, STOP and find a monkeypatch approach instead. The harness must be entirely additive.",

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: for ordinary pairs, the new diagnosis is not well-supported by the repo. the current sync effect in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/tools/travel-between-images/components/shotimageseditor/hooks/usesegmentslotmode.ts#l62) already syncs `url` and `primaryvariantid`, and the regenerate path reads exactly `starturl`, `endurl`, `startgenid`, `endgenid`, `startvariantid`, and `endvariantid` from `segmentslotmode.pairdata` in [/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts](/users/user_c042661f/documents/reigh-workspace/reigh-app/src/domains/media-lightbox/hooks/usevideoregeneratemode.ts#l438). adding `thumburl` and `generationid` to the sync does not, by itself, explain why an old image url is still being used for regular pairs. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

SpoofDbRuntime** \u2014 in-memory task graph supporting the full DB surface used by orchestrator handlers:\n- `add_task_to_db(payload, type, dependant_on)` \u2192 assigns UUID, stores task with status `Queued`, records dependencies, returns UUID\n- `get_orchestrator_child_tasks(orch_id)` \u2192 returns categorized dict of children matching the real return shape (`segments`, `stitch`, `join_clips_segment`, etc.)\n- `get_task_dependency(task_id)` \u2192 returns stored `dependant_on`\n- `get_task_current_status(task_id)` \u2192 returns current status string\n- `cancel_orchestrator_children(orch_id)` \u2192 sets all children to `Cancelled`\n- `cleanup_duplicate_child_tasks(orch_id)` \u2192 no-op\n- `get_task_output_location_from_db(task_id)` \u2192 returns stored output_location for completed tasks\n- `update_task_status(task_id, status, output)` \u2192 updates in-memory, logs `[SPOOF_DB]`\n- `update_task_status_supabase(task_id, status, output, thumbnail)` \u2192 same + logs `[SPOOF_DB] Complete:`\n- `reset_generation_started_at(task_id)` \u2192 no-op with debug log\n- `requeue_task_for_retry(task_id, ...)` \u2192 sets status back to `Queued`\n\nIMPORTANT: Read `source/core/db/task_completion.py`, `source/core/db/task_dependencies.py`, and `source/core/db/task_polling.py` to verify exact function signatures and return shapes before implementing. The orchestrator handlers depend on the exact dict structure returned by `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()` \u2014 get this wrong and orchestrators will crash.\n\n**2b. SpoofTaskFeed** replacing `poll_next_task`:\n- Holds `SpoofDbRuntime` reference\n- On poll: finds next ready task (status `Queued`, all dependencies satisfied)\n- Returns `(ClaimPollOutcome.CLAIMED, task_info)` for ready tasks\n- Returns `(ClaimPollOutcome.EMPTY, None)` for idle polls (configurable count between tasks, default: 1)\n- Returns `(None, None)` sentinel when all tasks are terminal and no more can become ready\n- Handles orchestrator child-task flow: root orchestators create children during processing \u2192 children become ready \u2192 feed serves them\n\n**2c.** Inject DB config: set `source.core.db.config.SUPABASE_URL = \"https://preview.supabase.local\"` and `SUPABASE_ACCESS_TOKEN = \"preview-token\"`.",

Grep for each function name to find all import sites.\n\n**6d. Main loop** mirroring `server.py:442-583`:\n```python\nbootstrap_runtime_environment()\nif args.debug:\n    enable_debug_mode()\nelse:\n    disable_debug_mode()\n\ndisplay = WorkerStatusDisplay(\"Preview GPU (spoofed)\", \"Preview Profile\")\ndisplay.show_banner()\nspoof_db = SpoofDbRuntime(fixtures)\nspoof_queue = SpoofQueue(wan_dir=wan2gp_path, db_runtime=spoof_db)\n\nwhile True:\n    outcome, task_info = poll_next_task(worker_id=\"preview-worker\", ...)\n    if outcome is None:  # sentinel: all tasks terminal\n        break\n    if outcome == ClaimPollOutcome.EMPTY:\n        display.show_idle()\n        time.sleep(0.5)  # compressed idle\n        continue\n    display.on_task_start()\n    raw_result = process_single_task(...)\n    # status logging mirrors server.py:496-547\n    # For orchestrator tasks: update SpoofDbRuntime so children become ready\n    display.on_task_done()\n```\n\n**6e. CLI flags:** `--task-types` (comma filter), `--debug`/`--normal` (default: normal), `--output-log PATH`, `--idle-polls N` (default: 1)\n\n**6f. Termination:** log summary and exit cleanly after feed exhausted.",

  "meta_commentary": "**Critical execution guidance:**\n\n**Import ordering is the single highest-risk aspect.** The wgp/torch/huggingface_hub stubs MUST be in sys.modules before ANY `from source.runtime.worker import server` or similar. The run_preview.py script should set up all stubs in a top-level block, then do all source/ imports inside a `def main()` or after the stub setup. If any source/ import happens at module level before stubs, server.py's `import wgp as wgp_mod` will fail.\n\n**Monkeypatching correctness is the second-highest risk.** Python's `from X import Y` binds `Y` in the importing module's namespace. Patching `X.Y` after import does nothing \u2014 you must patch `importing_module.Y`. For each of the ~18 monkeypatches, the executor MUST grep for the function name to find where it's imported and patch at that location. The most common mistake: patching `source.core.db.task_claim.poll_next_task` when server.py has already done `from source.core.db.task_claim import poll_next_task` \u2014 the server's local binding is unaffected.\n\n**SpoofDbRuntime is the most complex new component.** The orchestrator handlers (travel_orchestrator, join_clips_orchestrator) depend on exact dict shapes from `get_orchestrator_child_tasks()`. Read the handler code to see what keys it accesses. This is where the orchestrator flow will break if the shape is wrong.\n\n**SpoofQueue's 22-attribute surface:** Don't trust the plan's list blindly. Read task_processor.py:60-216 and task_processor.py:307-570 line by line, noting every `queue.X` or `self.X` (where self is the queue). Add any missing attributes. The plan's list is a starting point, not exhaustive.\n\n**Orchestrator child-task flow:** This is the most complex runtime behavior. After `travel_orchestrator` processes, it calls `add_task_to_db()` for children. SpoofDbRuntime stores them. SpoofTaskFeed must detect them on the next poll and serve them in dependency order. Then each child processes and completes. This multi-step flow is where integration bugs will concentrate.\n\n**Fixture accuracy:** Copy param structures from create_test_task.py exactly, then sanitize URLs. Don't invent params. Invalid param structures will cause silent failures or crashes deep in task processing.\n\n**No source/ modifications:** If you find yourself needing to change source/ files, STOP and find a monkeypatch approach instead. The harness must be entirely additive.",

(flagged 1 times across 1 plans)

- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the merge pseudocode does not actually sync 'all image fields' as claimed. it omits `id` and `position` from change detection, and when `fresh.startimage` or `fresh.endimage` becomes `null` it preserves the stale previous object instead of syncing that null state. as written, it also needs explicit null-guards around spreads of `prev.startimage` / `prev.endimage` to be safe in strict typescript. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: the plan's claim that `worker_state.py:200-204` (the 30-minute startup safety net) catches abandoned pods that exited before patching is slightly imprecise. line 200-204 only runs when `in_startup_phase` is true, which requires `startup_phase` to be in `('deps_installing', 'deps_verified', 'worker_starting')` per line 161. a pod that exits before its first patch has `startup_phase = none`, so `in_startup_phase` is false and the code falls through to the normal timeout checks (active_stale / not_claiming) at line 207+, not the 30-minute startup cap. both paths eventually reap the worker, so the end result is the same, but the overview's reasoning (naming 200-204 specifically) is wrong. worth correcting so future readers don't mis-reason about the invariant. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
- [DEBT] are-the-proposed-changes-technically-correct: are the proposed changes technically correct?: checked worker_state.py line 161 (`in_startup_phase = startup_phase in (...)`) against the plan's assumption that subsequent lenient writes are safe. if deps_installing succeeds but deps_verified silently fails, startup_phase stays at 'deps_installing' and the 30-minute cap from line 200-204 applies — even though the pod is actually progressing through `uv sync`. on a slow runpod image with cold wheel downloads, `uv sync --extra cuda124` plus wan2gp deps can take 20+ minutes. there's no concrete benchmark in the plan that says the 30-minute cap is safely above the expected sync time; if `uv sync` legitimately takes 25 minutes and the deps_verified patch fails on a transient network blip, the orchestrator will terminate a healthy pod. plan should either bump the cap or note that deps_verified swallowing is acceptable because the cap is generously above typical sync time. (flagged 1 times across 1 plans)
